ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e4415	Mr President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I want your attention 14 months and a few days back, on the 1st. Last May, the Federal Government invited a reform dialogue to the Hofburg to discuss the Austrian action programme on growth and employment. The following day, the “Salzburger Nachrichten” also commented: "Of course, one can hold a labour market summit on 1st. May be seen as a pure show, as this was done by representatives of the opposition in the run-up. But a comparison with our neighbouring country Germany shows that –... – it is always more sensible to discuss concrete measures on the day of work to create new jobs than to spread only class war slogans.” Ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] The very in-depth debates have essentially led to consensus that policy itself cannot create jobs, but they can develop framework conditions that allow businesses and companies to secure or expand jobs. Today, after more than 14 months, we may find that the seed of these ideas allows for a harvest of success; This is because we have not only started a qualification and employment offensive for more than 60,000 people – employment promotion for older people, the “flower model” for young people, qualification measures for women. There has never been such a budget for active labour market policy as this time: With a record budget of €1.8 billion, we have more than doubled this approach since 1999! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] This is the key success factor for these framework conditions that Austria is today a tangible model. The second is the tax reform, the largest tax reform in the Second Republic. We have thus relieved the low incomes, and we have ensured with the reduction of corporate tax to 25 percent that the farms stay in the country – and do not migrate! [[Objection to the SPÖ]] A great success of far-sighted action, ladies and gentlemen! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] The backbone of the Austrian economy – third aspect – is our small and medium-sized enterprises. Consciously, as employee representatives, I say: Two thirds of the unemployed have jobs in our small and medium-sized enterprises, and therefore the strengthening of these small and medium-sized enterprises and the maintenance of their independence is a command of the hour. A turnover of €290 billion and investment of €20 billion ensure employment, and therefore we have also perfectly supported the future opportunities of these colleagues in small and medium-sized enterprises; last but not least with the rounding out of the tax reform for revenue and expenditure calculators. Fourth: Large companies, international companies, some 1,000 headquarters have settled in our country because security, high qualifications of our employees, Austria, make an economic and opportunities hub also across the East. In Austria, we have the interesting phenomenon that some fear globalisation, although Austria is a clear winner of globalisation with its small and open economy. We are also, for example, the winners of enlargement, and that should be clearly punctuated. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] [[Call the SPÖ:And what about unemployment?]] In Slovenia, Bulgaria, Romania, Croatia, Serbia, Montenegro and Bosnia-Herzegovina, Austria is investor number one. The Economic Research Institute has calculated that the eastern opening and EU membership have brought up to 1 per cent of additional GDP growth in Austria – cumulated in approximately 150,000 employees. We in Austria are the winners of enlargement. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to point out something that sometimes goes down in daily news: Austria is also extremely attractive for foreign entrepreneurs. Foreign investment in Austria already amounts to just under €53 billion, thus securing 250 000 jobs. However, let us also bear in mind that more than half of the Austrian prosperity is already secured by exports. What we have not yet succeeded in football: In exports, we are European champions, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] [[Abg. May. Molterer: Come on!]] We should not put this common performance under the umbrella. Helmut Gansterer pointed out in his book “Good News”, “69 swallow vaccinations against black painters, pitys and other bazills”, precisely this achievement of Austria’s export-European championship. [[Abg. Dr. Stummful: You should give this to Matznetter!]] Wise economies celebrate such festivals because they motivate and lead to further success on their way over pride and confidence. It would be almost logical to immediately proclaim a “year of feasts ” and a“ year of export. ” Ladies and gentlemen, I plead that 2006 will at least be the year of the rich harvest. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP as well as on representatives of the Freeman – BZÖ.]] This is not only the Austrian findings, there are many foreign votes to do so. ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e7516	Mr President. High house! I think it would be useful, after six months of Austrian Presidency, to account for the highest body, the democratically elected National Council, which initiatives have been taken and which has been the result of this six months of intensive work. I would like to look back at the starting point. We had a terrible year last year. We had a horrendous star after the two failed referenda. No, the electorate in France and in Holland have said to the European Constitution. There was no agreement on the European Financial Regulation a year ago for the next seven years. In addition, there have been distortions of confidence of all kinds which have culminated, for example, in deciding on the start of the accession negotiations with Turkey, in respect of which an objective debate on the effects and consequences has at first been refused and only through the Austrian insistence on the ability to accept has shown a way of resolving this. But there have also been enormous tensions in specific legal projects, such as the reduced VAT rates, the reduction of highly harmful greenhouse gases, but also in the Services Directive. The word “Bolkestein” was practically a nightmare and proved to be a fissure, was not a hope, but was indeed a terror. Our aim was therefore to give more confidence, more clarity, more momentum for Europe, and this subject of trust was such a kind of Basso continuous in the work of our Presidency. The motto we set ourselves from the very beginning was: ‘Europe is listening’. That was also the title of our own homepage, which we set up in Austria. These three words, ‘Europe is listening’, have also begun the conclusions of the last European Council. Interesting was also the reaction of the public. Ten million accesses from all over Europe took place on this homepage. We have had 35 million access to the Presidency website of the Austrian Presidency, which shows that we have gone far beyond the occasion and far beyond the other subject that we are dealing with in closed circles or at conferences or mediated over newspaper reports refunds, and I think this is very positive and important. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] The second motto of our presidency was: Realism. In six months you cannot change the world, even the smaller world of Europe. Therefore, fundamental changes in direction are neither possible nor meaningful. We have not overestimated our possibilities or those of our partners. We have done our work with mind and mind. We have worked on a number of major construction sites in this semester, and I believe that we can also do some things in the constitutional debate, in the financial sector, in enlargement. In particular, we have opened up new topics, the energy debate, the migration and integration debate and the question of crisis management. At the beginning of the year, there was still a very narrow attitude in the constitutional debate. I can only remind you that in January, for example, the Dutch said that the Constitution was dead, that they were not at all willing to talk about it. We have worked very carefully from “Sound of Europe”, with invitation also from Barkende, to the Klosterneuburg Special Meeting of Foreign Ministers, and we have succeeded in breaking through silence in Klosterneuburg and in developing a choreography of the common steps for the future. It has been shown that the citizens of Europe want a Europe of results and not a navel show in the Constitutional Treaty. They want more transparency. They want better crisis management. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] We have therefore signed a very concrete roadmap for the future on these very issues. I have also tried very hard in the constitutional debate to strengthen the foreign ministers in particular, because we need ministers of Europe, foreign ministers who are central to this issue. Ladies and gentlemen! A special success has been achieved by Karl-Heinz Grasser, who succeeded in the financial perspective by Mrs Foreign Minister and State Secretary Finz. The result achieved by us for days and weeks can be seen. We have achieved a financial security with the European Parliament for the next seven years, and we can increase the funds specifically for the future-oriented programmes and projects. This means that the Community programmes can actually be implemented in a timely manner with the 1. Start January 2007. This gives us planning security, especially in rural areas, for which we have always been strongly committed – small Austrian success! While, unfortunately, the total budget for rural areas has been cut by 10 per cent, they are up by 10 per cent in Austria. Thank you, Josef Pröll, for your success in the negotiations. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] Ladies and gentlemen, on enlargement: A few weeks ago, some aspects of future enlargements were a taboo, such as the topic of acceptability. And now it is finally experiencing the proper attention. The citizens have brought this issue to the centre, and there it will remain, no matter what one or another says. Much has been discussed as to whether the eligibility as an enlargement criterion has been defined at the European Council or not. I think this is mirror fighting. Important is: Writtenly accepted by all in the negotiating mandate for Turkey, 3. On the Austrian proposal, on October 2005, the capacity to accept was made explicit on the criteria and conditions for future EU membership. This mandate has been adopted unanimously by the Council. The Minister for Foreign Affairs really did fundamentals. [[New congratulations to the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] It is new, at the last European Council, that the Commission will now present its own report for the first time, until late autumn, in order to define what reception capacity really means. And the question will arise individually, depending on the candidate countries. Ladies and gentlemen, of course this was a huge challenge also in the organisational field. We have had to hold 3 300 meetings. 16 EU Troika Foreign Ministers Meetings have been held, eleven Association Councils have been held, two Foreign Affairs Accession Conferences, Croatia and Turkey, a Council of Ministers with the African, Caribbean and Pacific States. We have held great summits, the EU-Japan Summit in Tokyo, one with Russia in Sochi, with the USA in Vienna and the great EU-Latin America-Caribbean Summit in Vienna. Of course, it is also sometimes asked whether the rotating presidency is actually successful. That's what I'm saying quite openly: As long as we do not have the Constitution with a new system, which will only have to prove itself in practice – I always had my doubts that then not even an institutionalized area of jealousy will create a kind of competition, but anyway – the Presidency is a good solution to really involve everyone and, above all, to ensure that, in certain foreign policy special formats, such as G 8, Middle East Quartet, Balkan Contact Group or EU-3 in the case of Iran, genuine involvement of all and the flow of information and opinions between Member States will work really well. It has really paid off for Austria that we have established a network of friends and contacts with the countries of the regional partnership, with particularly committed Member States, with the institutions in good time. Networking in good time has also been very worthwhile in very concrete things, for example at the Islam Conference in November 2005 here in Vienna, whose contacts and networks we have been able to benefit excellently from in the caricature crisis. And the reactions such as European Islam were thanks to God very different from in other parts of the world. I think this is an important, encouraging sign. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and the Freeman – BZÖ.]] Early contacts with the social partners, with the parliamentarians of the European Parliament and the Commission – and particularly with great thanks and respect for Martin Bartenstein – have resulted in the early establishment of those contacts which have ultimately led to one of the greatest successes, namely the solution to the Services Directive. Many points are open, but not because of the Austrian Presidency, but because of the current situation in the European Union. That's what you have to address. And it's right what the Federal Chancellor said: If the Presidency is held, then it is more difficult to bring national interests into the European debate than if this is not the case. This is what we expect now, and the Austrians also expect us to bring this Austrian position into the debate now, which is the future of the European Union. If, for example, Finland, the Finnish Presidency, does not think that the European Union's capacity to accept is an important priority, then I expect Austrian foreign policy to continue to support this principle, which we have rightly put in place within the framework of our Presidency: Yes, the European Union's capacity to accept further enlargement is an important criterion in deciding whether new Member States can be included in this Union. This principle must continue to be supported and pursued, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations with the liberals – BZÖ and representatives of the ÖVP.]] It is important that new candidate countries meet the criteria for membership. I have always said that – even that was not the decision of Austria: It was an error to open the accession negotiations with Turkey, although Turkey did not even meet the criterion that it would recognise all the Member States of the European Union. The European Union itself does not take European Union principles seriously. How can one then expect to be pressured on other countries to take over these values and to comply with them as well: Rule of law, respect for human rights, freedom of the press, women's rights? These are the values that we hold high in this European Union. How can we support the start of accession negotiations with a country that does not respect these basic values and is not prepared to respect these basic values? We have contradictions in Europe's policy, which certainly go in the wrong direction. [[Congratulations on the Freeman – BZÖ.]] On the constitutional question. – In Austria too, it is discussed: What do we need this Constitution for? Thank God they have been rejected! It will be time to think in other models for a common Europe that you say: If we want to include as many European countries as possible in this European family of the European Union and want to act more externally, for example within the framework of a common foreign policy, if we want to support a common defence, which would be a real advantage for smaller countries like Austria, then there will have to be a more flexible European Union, then it will be necessary to ensure that not all countries of the European Union can be equally deepened so that they cannot all be treated equally that, I do not want to say, different speeds will have to be different levels of integration. That is why we have proposed this model of the federal state of Europe – not a state such as the United States of America, but a European Union where each country decides for itself which integration models and modules it participates in. Then there will be a kind of core Europe – I hope Austria will be able to participate in this core Europe – which will take over the entire spectrum of these integration steps: Monetary, economic, security, defence, foreign policy. In addition, there will be other countries that cannot cover this entire spectrum. And in the extreme circle, there should be a partnership for Europe for all those countries that cannot or do not wish to participate in this Union as a full member. This would be an interesting perspective for the future, for a functioning Europe, where one does not go through treaties and summits with bureaucratic fins, where one does not have to discuss whether Parliament is going three times, four times or five times between Strasbourg and Brussels, but where it really is about creating future prospects for the functioning of this common Europe. Finally, the language in this European Union is a bit symbolic, if you are still proud that you speak a language that nobody understands, where you speak “in summits”: the Treaty of Amsterdam, the Treaty of Maastricht, the Petersberg tasks, the “JI” – who knows what the “JI” is? – the Justice and Home Affairs Council, and I still like BESOGEKO. At the time at BESOGEKO we decided this and that – no one understands that! The Summit on Employment, Social Affairs, Health and Consumer Protection is intended. As long as politicians, officials and bureaucrats are involved, no matter if they are understood by the population, we do not need to talk about whether there is more support for this European Union and for the important issues of the European Union in the population. [[Congratulations on the Freeman – BZÖ.]] We also do not need to discuss whether there should be European referendums or popular desires. These principles in the thinking of European politicians must be amended. That would be a first important step, and there could also be Austrian impulses. Thank you once again for your commitment to this presidency. An important step has been taken, important small steps have been taken, but we still have to wait for the important changes in the functioning of the European Union in the next 20, 30, 40 years, and we also expect Austrian impulses on this issue.

The second point that needs to be made is the point of enlargement. The second area with regard to Turkey's accession would have been the principles achieved by Austria at the autumn of last year, namely to take the right to be admitted as a criterion for Turkey's accession, but also to introduce a stop button which states that if there are difficulties in the negotiations with Turkey and if Turkey violates the essential principles of the European Union, it will have to press this stop button and there will be a stop in the negotiations with Turkey. The third point would have been to introduce a special scheme for Turkey in the event that it does not come to full accession. I have not heard all these three points in their importance during these six months of the presidency of our country. On the contrary: The capacity to accept as a criterion is broken down in the opinion of the other Member States. So she won't play a role anymore. The stop button, which has been announced, has not been pushed by anyone, although there would have been a hundred times the reason for this, because Turkey has proved to be not mature for the European Union and accession. And all these points point to the fact that the voice of the European population is not taken seriously in this area of enlargement. Ladies and gentlemen! Regardless of a presidency of our country, whether we are President or not, we must, if we want the European Union experiment to succeed, also represent these two principles in the coming years. There must be more proximity to the issue of creating a new European Constitution or a new European Constitutional Treaty and the issue of enlargement of the Union.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e10270	Mr Steindl has now come to speak. 3 minutes welcome. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e10291	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen in the House! I should like to begin by concurring fully with the comments made by my colleague Christine Marekan and perhaps also with those of my colleague Mr Bauer. It is true that the historical origin of the self-employed accountants goes very far back. Until 1948, these independent accountants had existed, then no longer until 1999. Since 1999 there have been two different accounting professions, the industrial accountants and the self-employed accountants, with different chamber memberships. It was therefore time to create a uniformly qualified balance sheet holder. This has also been achieved by this law. The new profession of the accountant comprises the previously separate professions of the commercial and the self-employed accountant. The demarcation to the tax advisor remains stable anyway. In addition, there are several sub-areas and qualifications such as those of the normal accountant and the wage calculator. Compromise solutions are always necessary in such far-reaching legislative decisions, and we have already heard where the main focus of this compromise is: On the one hand, this annual turnover limit of € 363 000 for accounting and, on the other hand, the exclusion of employee charges. Here it cannot be understood that personal calculators should not perform them. However, I am sure that in the future appropriate legal measures will follow in order to ultimately implement these compromises as they are necessary for practice. Overall, the economy needs these self-employed accountants and accountants very urgently. We now have around 2,500 self-employed accountants, who are highly qualified and adapted to the new matter doing their work, and I believe that the law here can also bring positive impetus.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e10751	Now we come to the 3rd. Agenda item. The first speaker was Mr. Ing. Winkler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e10776	Mr President. Estimated Minister of State! High house! In April 2006, on the basis of an infringement action, the ECJ found that measures to improve the safety and health of workers under the employment contract were not sufficiently implemented. The reason was that Directive 391 of 1989 was not fully transposed by the European Union. This amendment now adapts the Workers' Protection Act, the Labour Constitution and the Land Labor Act accordingly to the judgment. I am also pleased that these novelties will be adopted today, but they contain important points of employers' information obligations to the security trustees and to the works councils. Some sources of danger in daily work can often be stopped simply by talking about and informing about it. Information, advice and identification of sources of risk in advance help both workers and employers. Finally, any accident at work that could have been avoided – apart from the fact that each accident as such is too much – also causes costs for the holding and for the employee. In addition, in addition to personal suffering and health damage, longer sick people can often also cause job loss. However, I stress that there are many companies that have not waited for these provisions to be incorporated into the law but rather these points in practice already existing labour law and therefore probably less weighted to the EU than to the outside appearance. In addition to technical resources, the health of the workers and thus the labour force of the workers represents the real capital of the companies. It is therefore important not only to provide legal information, but also to continue continuing training of persons responsible for these tasks. Of course, technical progress is taking place everywhere, which is why we all – employers and workers – must strive to constantly use this knowledge in the field of accident prevention and thus the health of workers. However, the impression also arises that more is being invested in the technical equipment of companies and thus in machinery than in the health of employees. All the institutions involved in this field still have to work together in this basic setting, because, of course, if nothing changes, there can be nothing significant in front of the ECJ. Integrated works councils and security trustees, but also employers have already tried to identify and dissociate the sources of danger in the operation as far as possible. Finally, I should like to express my pleasure at the fact that the Land Labor Act was also included in the course of this amendment. Thank you very much, Minister of State.

Dr. Moser.]] In view of this ever-increasing network development, I understand, of course, the concern of those people living near mobile phones, whether their health is not affected or at risk. The question of possible health risks arising from electromagnetic fields derived from mobile communications installations is addressed by us. We really do not want to say this, Mrs Moser, that we have acted carelessly on this important issue. In the Telecommunications Act 2003 – you know this very well anyway – § 73 provides that, in the case of the establishment and operation of radio and telecommunications facilities, the protection of human life and health must be ensured. This is legally normized, please! As a standard – that is only to illustrate – the EU Council Recommendation serves to limit the exposure of the population to electromagnetic fields. And, ladies and gentlemen, Mrs Moser: The basis of this standard are the recommendations of the WHO and the International Commission on Non-Ionizing Radiation Protection, acting under the umbrella of WHO. The values set out therein were determined on the basis of the analysis of a wide range of scientific studies – i.e. not arbitrary or even determined by operators and founders – so values which are considered safe according to the current state of scientific knowledge. I would remind you, Mrs Moser, that all the criteria that have been taken into account help not to endanger the health of the population. That is why we have also chosen this procedure in the Telecommunications Act in 2003 to ensure that new scientific findings – which you will never mention by the Greens – can be responded quickly if possible. Compliance with the reference values of the EU Council Recommendation is constantly monitored by the bodies of the Telecommunications Authority. But you too, Mrs Moser, have not mentioned anything in your remarks. So if the suspicion exists – I repeat: the suspicion – that the required limit values are exceeded in a specific installation, this can be displayed at any time at the competent telecommunications office. These are all fully democratic institutions that protect the population, which can be used there. On the subject of research, a very important issue. Research in this field, Mrs Moser, says first of all: Clarification of health-related facts – and not any other area. It is important whether there are facts relevant to health, and then research must be carried out. However, I ask for understanding that this does not take place in the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology, but is primarily the Federal Ministry of Health and Women and the Federal Ministry for Agriculture, Forestry, Environment and Water Management. The Ministry of Life has the clear competence to develop legal bases with regard to limit values in the field of non-ionizing radiation. Nevertheless, we are of course taking this issue very seriously, and that is why – you have referred to it, honourable Member – we have also set up the Scientific Advisory Council on Radio, with the aim of providing scientific support for radio applications and non-ionizing radiation, mobile communications, broadcasting, direct radio and the like, but also power lines and microwave radiation. This Scientific Advisory Board on Radio – mentioning this But she never, Mrs Moser, is completely independent in her activity. The professional competence cannot be agreed upon by any member of this advisory board! What is the mission of this Scientific Advisory Council on Radio – you have never mentioned this before, Mrs Colleague? – that all published studies and all research that exists in this context are looked at, examined and conclusions drawn from scientific criteria. The Scientific Advisory Council on Radio has not only been set up once, so to speak, but will continue to deal with the issue of mobile communications and health in the future. If new studies are to come, these will of course be dealt with immediately by the Scientific Advisory Council on Radio. The previous findings – which must also be said – give reason for dewarning. After examination of all present scientific studies, including so-called critical studies, such as the ‘Reflex study’, the Scientific Advisory Council Funk concludes that there is no evidence of health risk in compliance with the limit values. I may also be able to make some of these recommendations to you, because I do not want to see any incorrect and false claims being made here in the room. For example, there will be a recommendation on the part of our house that an average view of shifts and changeovers will be assumed in the calendar year. This means that if a person does not always have shifts or changeovers in six days, but only four days in one month and eight days in the next month, for example, these months are at least counted as heavy work months, since the average is used in the calendar year. We will also approve the month of heavy work in part-time employment. This is particularly important, because especially in the care sector we have a large number of employees who, due to the seriousness of the activity, are working part-time. Moreover, the holiday is also counted as a heavy work, if you want to, that is, the holiday does not interrupt the heavy work, and that is also right and fair. We have also ensured that if someone aged 60 and over fulfils the conditions for the heavy-worker scheme, but for some reason for one or two more years is pursuing a light activity, the right to this heavy-worker pension is not lost. We will also ensure that people who have a low employment rate several times, including those who have a serious activity, are also covered by this heavy work scheme. Here I have just given you some essential recommendations, which are the result of an important working group which is now handed over to the main association, so that the main association in the implementation of this heavy-worker regulation can go through things very closely. Now there is the new regulation which we are probably discussing at second reading here, and which we have decided on. There is the non-contributory co-insurance for spouses and household members – including female and female colleagues, Mrs Lunacek – with the above-mentioned care obligations. This includes children's education, including family care from care level 4. A very correct thing, and it will remain because we have probably considered these things. For couples without the above-mentioned care obligations, there is the possibility of an additional contribution, i.e. a reduced contribution, and for communities. That is our direction of value, that is our position, we stand by, and we will acknowledge it again today. You can easily discuss your views with us, although not today, but there will be enough opportunity to do so if the choice goes well for you.

Wars are also in the 21st century. In the 19th century, no clean matter, even if weapons are used that are supposed to distinguish between friend and enemy and infrastructure and man, insinuating that military successes are to be achieved at the same time as human victims decline. Most of the suffering comes only when the troops and the television cameras have withdrawn and the interest and empathy of the public have faded. In particular, civilians are affected, and in particular children are affected because this army often looks like toys and there are a lot of victims. This scatter bombs and cluster union is primarily directed to destroy “soft targets”, as it is called in the military language to destroy people. They also have a very high blind passerby quota, and therefore often wait years after the fights on the ground fatal shots that react very sensitively to physical contact. In numerous wars, scatter bombs and army were used in large numbers, especially in the Afghanistan War, in the Gulf War, where about 50 million cluster unions were shot, where many civilians were killed in part years after. In Laos, for example, there are about 11,000 victims. After the Gulf War of 1991, up to 4,000 Iraqi civilians died of this ammunition. Only in the context of the fulfilment of Petersberg's tasks will this army be kept available in the camps of the Austrian federal army pro futuro. I believe that this would be important – and I would ask you to support this request – because a ban on only blind-gathering incidental ammunition includes a coherent commitment to functioning cluster union. We believe that this is a very cruel weapon, a terrible weapon that is forbidden. If the Austrian foreign policy continues to be a principle of disarmament, it should be put to an end if the principle of disarmament continues to exist. and felt obliged to provide humanitarian ideas. ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e18432	Madam President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Let me briefly say something about some of the agenda items before the negotiations – and then on the question of cluster ionization and scatter bombs for a longer time. The first point I would like to comment on is the statement on the withdrawal of the Austrian reservation on Article 11 of the Convention on the Elimination of All Forms of Discrimination of Women – CEDAW – with regard to the Night Labour of Women. As far as night work is concerned, we do not agree that there should be even greater protection and rights for workers, especially for women working at night. The background is that the staff members of the CEDAW Convention have also asked Austria to bring legalisation and approximation to EU law, and that is why we agree to this point, even though we are quite clear – I would like to stress this here once again – that even with regard to night work, increased protection measures are still necessary in order to improve the situation of women, but also of men working at night. That's what we're doing. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] My colleague Wolfgang Zinggl will speak on the issues of culture. On all other points, we are in agreement, with the exception of the last point, namely the report by the Committee on Foreign Affairs on our request concerning the ban on scatter bombs and clusters. Mr Posch has already made a few comments on this. I would like to add a few things to what was said yesterday at the poll organised by Greens and SPÖ by numerous experts. I was impressed yesterday. I already had enough information to support the request we have put forward, but the reports by experts from the International Committee of the Red Cross, Handicap International, from the Munition Coalition Cluster, i.e. the Kaolition against Scatter Bombs and Streumunition, have impressed me and, as I believe, the representative of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs, Mrs Auer, as well as the representative of the Ministry of Defence, Mr. Colonel Monsberger. I hope that, if already the government groups do not agree with our comprehensive request, this event yesterday was an event that will make a further step in this matter possible in the future. I would now like to refer to the two points to which there is no agreement from the government groups. One is a unilateral moratorium, where we believe that Austria should make such a statement, namely that we do not produce this ammunition – we do not do it anyway – but do not want to use it or to store it. This is the point where the Federal Ministry of Defense and the Federal Army say: No, we do not want such a moratorium, because we have a certain kind of assembly in store! We have the M 85 in stock, and we would like to be able to use them if we need them anywhere in foreign operations! So far, the Colonel of the Ministry of Defence has admitted that ammunition has never been used by anyone. So it was bought sometime, in the hope that there might also be top Petersberg operations, so peace-making operations and that you can use them as well. Yesterday, the legal expert of the International Committee on the Red Cross explained precisely that this ammunition is one that does not comply with international humanitarian law, namely the principle of proportionality. That means: What do you want to achieve in military terms, and how much destruction and suffering of the civilian population can be bought for it? This must have a certain relationship in a conflict, and this does not apply to the cluster union and the scatter bombs. An example of this has already been made by Mr Posch: Kosovo, June 1999, employs 295,000 pieces of this cluster union, which had been thrown into scatter bombs. 14 Serbian tanks were destroyed. 14, no more! Many of these 295,000 small things, which are still colorful and look like toys, are still there in part. This is not the case with the land mines, where you know when it is announced where they are located. This cluster ion is dropped in the form of bombs that contain many small ammunition parts. The entire area to which this ammunition falls is called “footprint” by experts in English, that is, “footprint”. It depends very much on how the soil is obtained where the ammunition falls: When it's hard, the ammunition stays up there. Many explode, but not all, one cannot see as a layman whether the ammunition is still sharp or not. But some are sinking when, for example, they fall on sand. When the soil is damp, they also sink. Then you don't know if any are below the surface or not. And when children walk around there and see these colorful things, yellow, red, blue, lying around, they attack them, take them up curiously – and then you can imagine what happens when these bombs have not previously exploded. That is why the experts say that this kind of ammunition is even more dangerous than the land mines. One of the employees of Handicap International, who was a member of the British Army for 25 years and who was himself victims of a land mine five years ago, namely, lost a leg, took place at our event on 11 October. July tells him that a friend once told him: If you survive a land mine you're climbing to, you're glad you survived! Then you lost one of your limbs, but you live! But if you become a victim of a scatter bomb, a cluster union, and you survive, that's quite different! Then it would have been better for some people if they had died. The friend showed him photos of people who have lost not only – the “only” is already said too much – an arm or a leg, but whose body has suffered the most massive injuries, because it is the splitter that hits and destroys the body, and these injuries are hardly cureable. For this reason, I was more impressed by yesterday's descriptions – you probably notice my opinion here – than by what I knew before, and I was even more convinced that it makes sense for Austria to decide a unilateral moratorium and that a total ban on the storage of this ammunition will be decided, not just the ammunition that has more than 1 per cent of the blind's rate. I would also like to explain why. Landmine Action's experts have explained that this is called "bomblets" in the professional language. The one who has the Austrian army in stock is the type M 85. The Federal Army claims that this has a blind-goat rate of only 1 per cent, where you can so to speak, that only very few of them explode and then endanger people. The British Ministry of Defence, not any NGO, has recently been the UK Ministry of Defence. In the course of their tests carried out in September 2005, it was noted that: “ Bomblets” showed a blindness rate of 2.3 percent. That's what the British Ministry of Defense says. Ladies and gentlemen of ÖVP and BZÖ, that is, if you decide today on your request, where Streumunition is not prohibited, where the blind pass rate is less than 1 percent, then it is actually the consequence of the fact that the Austrian federal army must destroy the existing ammunition of type M 85. I would just like to draw your attention to this, if you want to do this. You could also agree to our request. Then you would also have to destroy the existing cluster union, but then you would have at least a coherent attitude. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. May. Hakl.]] One last thing: It is said again and again – that was also the case with the representative of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and the representative of the Ministry of Defence yesterday – that Austria wants to do something very well. This is also reflected in the request made by the government groups: Austria should play a pioneering role. But it always says: If the others do something and if there is something at international, multilateral level, then we do more! There was a very clear line from the people of the Landmine Action Campaign. You remember: In 1995 there was finally a dynamic. The Ottawa Protocol was adopted in 1997. The people who were there back then said: Ten years ago, twelve years ago, many found: What these NGOs and these activists call for, namely the ban on landmines, anti-personnel mines, we do not need it all! This dynamic has changed. There's the Ottawa protocol. And the experts at the Landmine Action said quite clearly at yesterday's event: Waiting for something to happen at multilateral level is exactly the wrong way. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Amon.]] There is a need for action at national level. It is a good example of Belgian law: Austria could follow this. There is a need for action at national level, and whether it be small states, which are beginning and thus triggering a dynamic, so that it actually comes to a comprehensive ban on scatter bombs and clusters at some point. I think Austria should play a pioneering role there, as it has been the case with the landmine campaign – and not to wait for the others!

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e18572	Madam President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I would like to make two brief comments on the points dealt with here under one. First of all, on the very detailed question raised by Mrs Lunacek, the initiative for a ban on scatter bombs and streumunition. Mrs Lunacek and Mr Posch: Of course, we fully share these humanitarian objectives. I should like to remind you that since the attempt to ban landmines, Austrian foreign policy has always been very active in disarmament and arms control since there was a foreign ministry and has always played a pioneering role. I recall the seventies when the Convention on Conventional Weapons was prepared with the additional protocols. In the meantime, we are in Protocol V, where Austrian delegates played a very important role. It belongs, as it were, to the standard repertoire of Austrian foreign policy – among all governments – that we are very actively dealing with the question of the further development of international humanitarian law. That is why we are very grateful for this initiative, because this is an important concern for us too. It is about avoiding unnecessary suffering in war too. I think we all realistically realize that God's wars are still there. But we must do everything we can to ensure that, if there are wars, the sufferings of people are kept as low as possible. The question is – and I think we differ from each other –: How does the best approach look? – It is not so, Mrs Lunacek, that we do not comply with the call for a total ban here because we would be, as it were, too cowardly or wait for others to take over this role. We have also committed ourselves to a ban on landmines from the very beginning. The whole thing finally came from civil society, and the government has taken this up. The view of persons described by you: Well, we don't take everything so seriously, what do they want?, which at least never existed in the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. I can assure you. There is also no opinion now that one should not work on a ban in the longer term, but I think we have to consider what is more reasonable: to achieve nothing or to move forward in steps for a few years, and at least to reduce the risk – you have described this very impressively – that of non-detonated ammunition. That is why we are trying to achieve, in the context of the negotiations on another Additional Protocol to the Convention on Arms Agreement, rules that prevent the use of cluster ammunition at such high blindness rates. This seems to me to be a rather reasonable strategic process. We need to take note – this has nothing to do with the fact that we do not want to – that a total ban today is politically simple and simply not feasible and that there is still a lot of work to be done by governments, but also civil society. I very much welcome this if civil society is committed to doing so, because it also increases the pressure in public opinion that will then lead governments to act. But we must be realistic and first try to strengthen humanitarian awareness with like-minded states. In our opinion, this can only be done in an addition to the Waffen Convention and must also include – that is very important – the important military powers. We should therefore work to avoid explosive residues of combat and thus the danger you have described to the civilian population. As to whether Austria will now ratify Protocol V, I can say that, for a few days now, all language versions have been available and that is why it is likely to be decided in the August Council of Ministers to forward them to the House. We will make great efforts, and I hope that this can be approved very soon by the National Council. We are also very much committed to a protocol VI which has exactly this humanitarian effect of reducing the source of danger to the civilian population. I believe that this is a reasonable, realistic and quite appropriate way. I would just like to say a few words about the agreement with Albania on cooperation in the fields of culture, education and science mentioned in a contribution to the debate. In the light of what we have already discussed in connection with the Austrian Presidency, namely the Balkans, it seems to me to be essential to note that the symbolic importance of this agreement goes far beyond the occasion. This is an agreement we have with many states, it is so to speak, not a ‘world-moving’ symbol, but a very clear symbol of a state that has not lived in such a total isolation for so many years, as can only be compared to North Korea today; however, Albania has not fired missiles, so they have behaved a little better. Albania has made tremendous progress. People are – you can see that when you talk to them – full of European enthusiasm. It is therefore important that we show a sign in the field of cooperation with universities, with young people in vocational training, in the school sector. The budget approaches provided for are not very high to God, they are also more symbolic, but they are rather minimal approaches, which can also be increased. But this agreement is particularly important for the country, for the integration and return of Albania to Europe. - Thank you.

At the heart of this Convention is the right of every state to take regulatory and financial measures to protect and promote the diversity of cultural forms in its territory. At the same time, the exchange of artistic ideas should be promoted. In short: It is about the speciality of cultural goods and services, as progressive liberalisation makes the protection and promotion of the diversity of cultural forms of expression particularly important. National cultural policy, public cultural promotion, must be strengthened against possible restrictions on competition. I believe that the challenges will be very diverse at national level in particular. I am thinking here, for example, of increasing trends in making use of the Internet and cyberspace a charge. There will not only be an increased demand for cultural policy, but also for justice and media policy. In order for this Agreement to enter into force, at least 30 ratifications shall be necessary by June 2007. The first four ratifications – that is the state of June of this year – take place through Canada, Mauritius, Mexico and Burkina Faso. It is very positive – I have already mentioned in the Committee – that Austria will be one of the first EU countries to ratify this Convention. The pace at which the Member States' approach to this ratification process is also an important indicator of the importance and seriousness of this Convention. I am convinced that the issues of cultural diversity – as we have already experienced in the discussion today, but beyond that – and also the issues of cultural rights in view of globalisation will remain key issues in the coming years. The success of this Agreement will depend on the use of the instruments of this Convention and the compliance with agreed criteria for the protection and promotion of cultural diversity, for example in the WTO negotiations. Dr. Maria Theresia Fekter, colleagues on a Federal Law on the Cooperation of Authorities in Consumer Protection (Consumer Authority Cooperation Act – VBKG) (1615 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e19631	This leads us to the 15th place. Agenda item. First speaker is Mrs Dr. Fekter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e19656	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! Consumer Authority Cooperation Act. This is an EU directive transposition, and it is about the fact that, when cross-border consumer protection violations have occurred, foreign authorities must be able to have a contact partner or a contact partner known under the legislation. [[Abg. May. Worm: Contact!]] – Contact person, thank you, Mrs Wurm, you are of course right. In Austria, consumer protection is competently distributed to several ministries: the legal system in the judicial sector, mainly in the social sector of consumer protection in the real sense, the food in the Ministry of Agriculture and the Ministry of Economic Affairs, the Federal Competition Authority. In order for this to be bundled, clarity has been created here. It has been established to whom a foreign authority can address in the event of a consumer protection infringement. It should be noted that the principle of proportionality is very prominent and clear in this law. That is to say, it must be examined that the authority should only intervene in rights insofar as this is provided for by law – think of professional secrets et cetera – and that, in order to fulfil the tasks, only the necessary measures must be taken and must not be carried out in excess. There are powers of investigation which are justified only if there is a reasonable suspicion. Then there are information rights, the right to obtain documents or also the right to keep an eye on companies. If these rights are denied, so no information is provided or the documents are not extracted or the suspect consumer protection infringers – I say now – do not allow consideration, then the court is required, and then it will be necessary for a court procedure to provide evidence in order to order court to conduct house searches. I mentioned this explicitly here because the EU does not always take full account of our legal protection system and does not always take into account that in Austria you can carry out a house search, an examination only with a house search warrant issued by the court. There is also the possibility that the infringements will be reversed by interim orders or, where damage has occurred, by court proceedings. I believe that it creates clarity with regard to the diversity of activity that takes place within the framework of Austrian consumer protection in the ministries. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2006-07-12-022-XXII-NRSITZ-00158_d7e22003	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! We have already discussed the heavy-worker regulation under item 6 of the agenda today. It is a difficult matter because there is no generally valid definition. One had to rely on the Night Heavy Labour Act, and the findings of occupational medicine and know-how were also included. It is new country, as the Federal Minister has said this afternoon, not only in Austria, but also internationally. That is why it is also important that an expert commission, set up in the Federal Ministry, regularly evaluate this heavy-work pension scheme. With this law there is now a heavy-work pension scheme for all occupational groups, and it is important that the public service is now also included in this heavy-worker pension. Thus, all officials will be covered by this scheme in the future if they have done at least ten years of serious work in the last 20 years before retirement. At the same time, a restable total service period of 42 years after the 18th century must be completed. ==References== Those who meet these conditions will have at least 60 years of age. Year of life entitled to early retirement. I can also say that the heavy workers have a much lower discount than non-slight workers, i.e. 1.8 percent instead of 4.2 percent. The public service has now also succeeded in including those areas which have a high risk to life and life in their professional field of activity. It is the executive officials or colleagues who are involved in the defence of the state. I think it is a way in the right direction, and on this legislative basis we will be able to further develop the heavy work pension in the next few years. Thank you.
